# Project test cleanup: project3

Pre-edit audit recorded before modifications. Scope: every test definition/fuzz-target family in the 34 assigned files: 409 original decision rows, comprising 373 named declarations, 35 fuzz targets and one additional dynamic roll family. See [counting basis](counting-basis.md). Parameterized families share a row only when they assert the same contract. Cleanup implementation is complete; runtime validation is explicitly pending the root integration gate. Dispositions record pre-edit decisions and the documented cross-review corrections.

For isolated mathematical units, candidate failures were: wrong standard coefficients/signs, lost boundary values, incorrect time/coordinate mapping, numeric instability, malformed-input crashes, and destructive state changes. Retain only cases that detect one of those observable failures. Registry declarations, internal packing, arbitrary sample counts, object identities and self-comparisons are not contracts.

Each retained/reworked row states all six bars explicitly: independent requirement (1), observable failure (2), oracle (3), stable seam (4), refactor tolerance (5), unique ownership (6). Non-test callers and risk/commands are recorded with each file. Read source owners alongside each test before choosing the disposition; source path names below identify areas, not new files to create.

## Validation and removed support

Completed cleanup, not audit-only: **65 DELETE, 25 REWRITE, 319 KEEP** across **409 test definitions/fuzz-target families in 34 assigned files**. Parameterized families are counted once; this is not a runtime-test count. Cross-review retained selector-domain properties after identifying untested empty/singleton lengths, asymmetric limits and ease/combination input ranges beyond the external oracle examples.

Removed support:

- Catalog name/default arrays, registry/default lookup helpers and empty suites in the three effect test files; normalizer differential runner, generated command stream, synthetic private HandlerContext helpers and `setDeltaScans` imports; copied FilmCraft scene detector, benchmark comparison and optional `SCENE_REPORT` logging; dead text preset inventory, phantom `_V` type export and unused imports.
- Deleted `cutAccuracy` from `packages/project/src/time-remap/scenes.ts`: all consumers were tests; project3 now compares authored scene cuts directly and engine2 confirmed its `apps/engine/test/time-export.test.ts` caller changes to authored `[45,75,120,198]` cut frames.
- Deleted dead `shutterTimes` from `registry/fx/sapphire-distort-warp/math.ts`; no production/documentation caller existed.
- Made implementation-only exports private after checking all packages, apps, scripts and documentation: `tonalWeights`, `skinWeight`; `lensPasses`, `dropGrid`, `shakeParamsOf`, `shakeNoise`; `freezeOffset`, `filmDamageSignals`, `flashMotion`, `grungeStyle`, `grungeLayerUniforms`, `GRAIN_PRESETS`, `grainSettings`, `tvRoll`; `SPLINE_N`, `zapTau`, `zapSeed`, `glowGain`. Their same-module production calls remain unchanged.
- Also privatized `warpShutterParams`, `perspectiveHit`, `perspectiveProject` in coordination with project1/project2, which removed their sole external test callers. Root must integrate those test changes before package typecheck.
- Kept actual cross-module APIs including `resolveCachedLayerProps` (composition renderers), `digitalDamageGates` and `shakeUniforms` (transitions), `E_GREY`/`NEG_RANGE` (film shader), `thinPoints` (audio commands), and `processedItemName` (process command).
- Project2 owns removal of `setDeltaScans` itself after this commit clears project3 callers. No fixture/snapshot files were orphaned; external Rust selector fixtures remain used. Removed the stale render-family source comment claiming a catalog test still exists.

Validation actually performed:

- `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline` completed in this worktree.
- `pnpm exec biome check --write <31 changed TypeScript files>` passed; only scoped import/format changes were applied.
- `git diff --check` passed; production and test diffs were reviewed.
- The 34-file focused baseline command started with `--maxWorkers=1`, but produced no test-case verdict before the root agent requested its termination due shared-host overload. It is **not** reported as passed or as a product failure.
- Focused post-cleanup tests, `pnpm --filter @jasper/project typecheck`, repository lint/typecheck/tests/build and specialized GL/E2E validation are **pending root integration** under the parent’s resource coordination. No coverage/count gate was weakened. No baseline product failure was observed in the no-verdict run.

Integration dependencies: project1 `fx-framework-followups.test.ts`, project2 `fx-motion-blur.test.ts` and test-only delta flag cleanup, engine2 `time-export.test.ts`. Production executable behavior is unchanged; removals are dead test support and private-export visibility only.

## Final oracle correction (recorded before the test edits)

The targeted follow-up reviewed retained assertions against integration `cf890a29`, including imported constants and helper-derived expectations. Public timebase/config/protocol constants and independent preservation properties remain valid; importing an API alone is not a reason to delete its test. This correction changes four existing test definitions in three files, with no new test definitions or production changes.

- **REWRITE — `packages/project/test/sim-warp-commands.test.ts`: “moves vertices and tangents with stopwatch semantics; validates indices”.** The old reset expectation calls `meshWarpRest`, exactly the generator called by the command, so both sides can share a broken grid. The length comparison only restates that generator. **1:** `docs/wp-notes/sim-warp.md` describes persisted normalized Bézier-patch vertices/tangents and reset; a regular two-cell straight cubic grid independently places its center at `[0.5, 0.5]` with a right tangent of `[1/6, 0]`. **2:** A reset that leaves the edited position/tangent or writes incorrect coordinates visibly deforms the layer; invalid indices and empty edits must still be refused. **3:** The replacement uses explicitly configured rows/columns and authored mathematical coordinates, never the production generator as an oracle. **4:** It calls public commands and reads persisted mesh data, whose flat vertex format is part of saved project state. **5:** Grid generation and command internals can be refactored without changing this geometry or refusal behavior. **6:** The command test uniquely checks reset after an edit and command validation; GL rest-image checks own rendered identity but cannot prove this command writes the reset. Non-test callers of `meshWarpRest` remain the reset handler, mesh normalization/rendering and the web warp overlay; its export remains. Risk: low, test-only setup/oracle change. Validation: scoped Biome and diff review now; focused `pnpm exec vitest run packages/project/test/sim-warp-commands.test.ts --maxWorkers=1` and package typecheck deferred to root’s integrated validation.

- **REWRITE — `packages/project/test/imports-interpret.test.ts`: “pixel variant keys round-trip (property)”.** Encode/decode/encode equality can pass when serialization silently loses the original interpretation; the arbitrary-string property repeats `parsePixelVariant`’s own `pixelVariantKey(out) === text` guard. **1:** `docs/wp-notes/imports.md` specifies interpreted field order and alpha mode/matte/inversion, canonical derivative keys and normalization of default straight alpha. **2:** Lost or colliding settings select the wrong cached/proxied picture or alter interpreted alpha. **3:** Decode the generated key and compare semantic fields against the original generated inputs, treating omitted defaults explicitly and comparing matte only when premultiplied; no expected value calls the encoder. **4:** Encoder/parser are the public URL/job/cache protocol seam. **5:** Equivalent spelling-preserving encoder/parser refactors pass even when normalization internals change. **6:** This is the lowest serialized-interpretation owner: engine pixel tests cover a few effects but do not cover all mode/inversion/matte and field-order combinations. Keep the two authored malformed examples; remove the canonicality self-check. Non-test callers include engine `imports/variants.ts`, project `render/snapshot.ts` and export `retime/extension.ts`; both exports remain. Risk: low; normalization of unused matte and default straight alpha is deliberately respected. Validation: scoped Biome/diff now; focused imports interpretation suite and package typecheck deferred to root.

- **REWRITE — `packages/project/test/masks-path.test.ts`: “flattens an ellipse onto the ellipse”.** The original minimum sample count has no independent requirement. **1:** `docs/wp-notes/masks.md` requires flattened mask outlines with bounded error; the analytic ellipse equation and area specify this fixture independently. **2:** Empty output removes the visible mask; off-curve vertices or wrong area distort it. **3:** Expected geometry comes from ellipse mathematics and the authored radii, with a nonempty guard preventing a vacuous point loop. **4:** `flattenPath` and `pathSignedArea` expose the mask geometry consumed by renderers. **5:** Adaptive subdivision may choose a different sample count without breaking the assertion. **6:** This is the lowest curve-geometry owner; command and raster integration tests do not independently establish this analytic curve fixture. Both geometry APIs have production renderer/geometry callers and remain exported. Risk: low, removing an arbitrary count only. Validation: scoped Biome/diff now; masks-path suite and package typecheck deferred to root.

- **REWRITE — `packages/project/test/masks-path.test.ts`: “Mask Path keyframes: vertex-wise for equal counts, smart matching otherwise”.** The old four-vertex intermediate census fixes one correspondence implementation, and `toBe(tri)` requires an object-identity optimization. **1:** `docs/wp-notes/masks.md` specifies vertex-wise equal-count interpolation, smart matching for different counts and keyframe timing. **2:** A held or nonfinite in-between freezes/corrupts the animated mask, and changed exact-key geometry alters the authored shape. **3:** Keep authored translated-rectangle midpoint coordinates and compare keyed values with the authored input shapes; differing-count intermediates must be finite and differ from both keyed shapes. **4:** Public `evaluate` is the animation consumer seam. **5:** Correspondence, output tessellation and object allocation can change while semantic geometry and timing remain valid. **6:** Direct correspondence/rigid-geometry cases in the same file own geometric matching; this case uniquely verifies keyframe dispatch/timing and endpoint preservation, so the duplicate direct `lerpPathValues` probe is removed. `lerpPathValues` remains used by production keyframe evaluation and remains exported; only its unused test import is removed. Risk: low; no production behavior changes. Validation: scoped Biome/diff now; masks-path suite and package typecheck deferred to root.

Follow-up validation completed: `pnpm exec biome check --write packages/project/test/sim-warp-commands.test.ts packages/project/test/imports-interpret.test.ts packages/project/test/masks-path.test.ts` passed with no fixes; `git diff --check` passed; the complete four-file diff was reviewed. No runtime checks are claimed by this follow-up while the parent coordinates shared-host resources. The three test files have zero net LOC change; the overall cleanup remains net-negative. No additional production seam or fixture was removed.


## Untrusted pixel-variant parser correction (recorded before editing)

**REWRITE continuation — `packages/project/test/imports-interpret.test.ts`, the pixel-variant property family.** The final oracle correction correctly removed encode/parse/encode and the parser’s self-canonicality assertion, but deleting the arbitrary-text generator also removed the sole malformed-input fuzz domain. Before editing, the owner `interpret/model.ts` and its URL/job caller `apps/engine/src/imports/variants.ts` were re-read; the latter expects `null` rejection so it can return `BAD_REQUEST`, rather than an untyped parser exception. Failure modes are an exception on arbitrary text or accepted output with invalid field-order/alpha values, nonboolean inversion, or a nonfinite/out-of-range RGB matte. **1:** AGENTS.md requires parser fuzzing and visible errors instead of crashes; the public `PixelInterpretation` contract and `docs/wp-notes/imports.md` define optional upper/lower field order, straight/premultiplied/ignore alpha, RGB matte in [0,1], and inversion. **2:** An exception bypasses the caller’s expected rejection path; malformed accepted settings can produce invalid decoding parameters or wrong pictures. **3:** Restore an arbitrary-text property whose expected values are literal permitted enums, boolean inversion and finite bounded three-channel matte; it never invokes the encoder to validate parser output. Authored valid protocol examples exercise the accepted-output branch as well as random malformed text. **4:** Call the public parser at the URL/job boundary. **5:** Tokenization, allocation, canonicalization and helper changes remain unconstrained when returned semantic values remain valid. **6:** The semantic serialization property covers generated valid interpretations, while this property uniquely covers arbitrary malformed text; engine variant examples do not replace that parser input domain. Both encoder and parser remain real production APIs; no seam or fixture is removed. Risk: low, test-only restoration with an independent oracle.
